Existing OVIS methods usually rely on vision-language alignment, where visual region features are matched with textual category embeddings to recognize both seen and unseen classes. However, this alignment assumption becomes fragile in underwater environments. Color attenuation, scattering, turbidity, and low contrast distort visual appearance, causing region features to deviate from their corresponding semantic concepts. As a result, the similarity between degraded visual features and textual classifiers becomes unreliable, which particularly weakens the recognition of unseen underwater categories that cannot be corrected by category-specific supervision. To address these issues, we propose GeoPrompt, a geometry-conditioned prompt learning framework for underwater open-vocabulary instance segmentation. Our key insight is that geometric structure is more stable than visual appearance in degraded underwater scenes. Specifically, GeoPrompt introduces a geometry-guided visual adaptation module (GVAM) to extract depth-derived structural priors and inject them into multi-scale visual features. It further develops a geometry-conditioned prompt learning (GCPL) that generates geometry-aware textual classifiers from structural representations. These geometry-aware classifiers are integrated with fixed and image-conditioned textual classifiers to improve open-vocabulary semantic alignment. Experiments on the MARIS-OpenIS benchmark show that GeoPrompt achieves state-of-the-art performance and brings consistent improvements on underwater open-vocabulary instance segmentation.
  </details>

- **[StickFigure: Enabling Pose-Only IoT Cameras for Privacy-Preserving Edge Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34130)**  `arXiv:2610.34130`  `cs.CV`  
  _Aveline Xiaoyuan Han, Chenshu Wu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Indoor IoT cameras can support assisted living and activity-aware spaces, but exporting raw RGB frames raises privacy concerns by exposing facial, clothing, and household details that body-motion tasks often do not require. We present StickFigure, a pose-only IoT camera system that, during real-time monitoring, keeps raw frames local and ephemeral while exporting only compact skeleton streams sufficient for downstream human activity recognition (HAR). While pose estimation has matured on GPUs, extracting accurate pose streams on resource-constrained IoT cameras remains difficult. To overcome these challenges and enable on-device pose streams on (almost) any IoT camera, we present two core designs in StickFigure: an activation-aware pose backbone that allocates channel capacity after spatial downsampling and a lightweight hierarchical MLP refiner that selectively corrects elbow and wrist coordinates using intermediate visual features and kinematic context. We fully implement StickFigure and evaluate it on public benchmarks and real-world deployment. Compared with state-of-the-art MCU pose models, StickFigure achieves the highest pose accuracy and downstream HAR utility on both NTU RGB+D and Penn Action, while using 96.5% fewer parameters and 99.3% fewer MACs per frame than RTMPose-S-based baselines, achieving 0.107 s p95 latency on a representative STM32H753 MCU. We believe StickFigure establishes a new operating point for privacy-aware edge vision on massive IoT cameras.
  </details>

- **[LegoOcc++: Towards Consistent Open-Vocabulary 3D Occupancy Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34093)**  `arXiv:2610.34093`  `cs.CV`  
  _Changqing Zhou, Yueru Luo, Han Zhang, Zeyu Jiang, Changhao Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Open-vocabulary 3D occupancy prediction is important for embodied agents operating in complex environments whose semantic categories extend beyond fixed taxonomies. While recent studies have explored open-vocabulary occupancy in outdoor driving scenarios, indoor environments remain challenging due to dense occlusions, complex geometry, and fine-grained long-tailed semantics. To reduce the need for expensive voxel-level semantic annotations, we study a geometry-only supervision setting requiring only binary occupancy labels. We build upon Language-Embedded Gaussians as a unified representation coupling 3D geometry with language-aligned semantics. For geometry learning, we introduce an opacity-aware Poisson-based Gaussian-to-Occupancy formulation that enables stable volumetric aggregation under binary occupancy supervision. For semantic learning, we render Gaussian features into images and align them with a frozen open-vocabulary model, together with Progressive Temperature Decay to reduce feature mixing during Gaussian splatting. Building on this framework, we further introduce Instance-Level Semantic Consistency to alleviate fragmented object predictions, and Covisibility-Aware Support-View Selection to provide informative and less redundant multi-view semantic supervision. We also extend our framework to outdoor driving scenarios to evaluate its generality across substantially different environments. Extensive experiments on indoor and outdoor benchmarks demonstrate that our method achieves strong open-vocabulary occupancy performance, validating the effectiveness and generality of our framework. Code will be available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Learning the Autoregressive: Direct Score-Difference Estimation with Amortized Future Credit for Video Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34092)**  `arXiv:2610.34092`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zixuan Duan, Yabo Chen, Xunzhi Xiang, Xin Zhang, Haibin Huang, Yin Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Few-step distillation for autoregressive (AR) video generation commonly first initializes an AR student and then refines it with distribution matching distillation (DMD). This refinement process faces two challenges. First, although initialization already yields a reasonably capable student, DMD obtains the refinement direction indirectly by estimating the full student score before forming the teacher-student correction. Second, detached-history training prevents the generator from assigning credit to a current chunk for its influence on subsequent chunks. We introduce Leta, a few-step distillation framework that addresses these challenges with ScoreDelta and FutureCredit. ScoreDelta directly estimates the teacher-student correction from denoising residuals, while FutureCredit learns an auxiliary cross-chunk update signal from vector-Jacobian products (VJPs) of next-chunk teacher residuals. Leta combines these signals into a joint teacher-guided update field in a single forward pass during generator updates. Teacher evaluations and credit VJPs are used only for estimator training. For ScoreDelta, we establish population equivalence to score-difference estimation and a conditional approximation advantage under limited capacity in a fixed-feature linear model. In our controlled experiments, Leta improves over DMD by 0.9 points on VBench and 0.66 on VideoAlign. This advantage persists at a higher generator update frequency (a generator-to-estimator update ratio of G:E=1:1), where Leta improves over DMD by 1.6 on VBench and 0.35 on VideoAlign. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[What Sketches Tell Us about LVLMs: Conventions, Grounding, and Localisation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34037)**  `arXiv:2610.34037`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chaitat Utintu, Ahmed Bourouis, Arkaprabha Basu, Yi-Zhe Song_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  LVLM benchmarks tell us whether a model answers correctly, but not which visual conventions it has retained, nor where those conventions live. Sketches expose this hidden structure. A sketch removes texture, lighting, and photographic context while preserving the strokes humans judged sufficient for recognition. If an LVLM can operate on that abstraction, the surviving signal is structural rather than merely photometric. We introduce a training-free sketch probe with three readouts: temporal part-construction order, spatial attention-based grounding, and the mechanistic layer/head locus of grounding. We evaluate nine LVLMs on part order and five on the attention readouts, over two part-annotated sketch datasets. Three findings emerge. First, LVLM part order tracks human convention: models reproduce canonical orderings where human drawings converge, and become correspondingly variable where human orderings vary. Controls that blank, withhold, and mismatch the sketch locate this convention in the language model. Second, part-level grounding survives the abstraction: attention heads selected from the frozen model with an existing recipe recover part and object regions on object and scene sketches, with no grounding supervision. Third, the grounding signal concentrates in a narrow mid-network band whose location follows the base language model rather than parameter count in the two families we compare, and remains stable across object and scene sketches. The result is a practical diagnostic for sketch-driven LVLMs: no fine-tuning, no auxiliary segmenter, and a layer band that doubles as a head-selection prior, matching or improving grounding mAcc@0.1 in every model and dataset we tested.
  </details>

- **[Fovea-JEPA: Overcoming Geometric Blind Spots in V-JEPA for Camera Motion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34033)**  `arXiv:2610.34033`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiangkun Shi, Hanhui Li, Shiyue Zhang, Chang Liu, Junwen Pan, Zhenbang Sun, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Camera motion recognition requires separating viewpoint changes caused by camera translation, rotation, or zoom from object motion and scene dynamics. Video-language models often rely on semantic cues and struggle with the geometric evidence needed for this task. Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures such as V-JEPA offer strong spatio-temporal representations, but our experiments suggest that they underrepresent geometric cues in some regions. Under temporal-reversal supervision, V-JEPA improves overall but degrades on several motion families, whereas a weaker geometry-oriented VGGT predictor improves across all families. We refer to these geometrically underrepresented regions as geometric blind spots and propose Fovea-JEPA to identify and address them. Using V-JEPA as the base model and VGGT as a geometry expert, we construct intervention pseudo-labels from their prediction errors to weakly supervise a blind-spot predictor. The predictor produces token-wise response maps, which are aggregated into a clip-level intervention score to guide adaptive fusion of visual and geometric features. This selective intervention introduces complementary geometric information while preserving reliable visual predictions. Experiments on CameraMotionDataset, CameraBench, and ShotBench demonstrate the effectiveness of Fovea-JEPA, with gains of 9.67, 8.67, and 24.38 percentage points over previously reported state-of-the-art baselines, respectively. Code and models will be released upon acceptance.
  </details>

- **[Explaining Navigation Decisions: Stage-Aware Visual Attribution for Vision-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34028)**  `arXiv:2610.34028`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yiyuan Yao, Xinpeng Ding, Minglei Chen, Fei Gao, Lihuo He, Jie Yuan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As Vision-Language Models (VLMs) increasingly enable robots to follow language instructions, understanding their navigation decisions becomes important for reliable deployment. Existing explainability methods overlook a key challenge in Vision-Language Navigation (VLN): evidence for the current action may be distributed across historical and current observations and associated with different instruction stages. We identify instruction-stage insensitivity, where existing methods produce similar explanations despite changes across different navigation stages. To address this issue, we propose a stage-aware attribution framework for VLN. We first construct action-conditioned temporal instruction--visual attributions across observations. These attributions are projected into instruction space to estimate stage associations, which guide the aggregation of relevant historical evidence for refining the current explanation. An instruction-guided spatial filter further suppresses isolated responses while preserving compact, instruction-relevant regions. Experiments across multiple VLN policies and benchmarks show improved attribution quality over existing methods. We further demonstrate that our method acts as a diagnostic tool for analyzing failure cases, and validate its applicability to real-world navigation on a Unitree G1 robot.
  </details>

- **[Accuracy Is Not Enough: An Efficiency- and Statistics-Aware Benchmark of ResNet50 and Vision Transformer for Explainable Multi-Class Gastrointestinal Diseases Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33965)**  `arXiv:2610.33965`  `cs.CV`  
  _Akwasi Asare_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automated classification of gastrointestinal (GI) findings in endoscopic images could support early diagnosis, but architectures are often chosen on accuracy alone. We present a controlled, reproducible benchmark of ResNet50 and ViT-B/16 for eight-class GI classification on the public Kvasir dataset (4,000 images), using a stratified split (400 test images) and stratified group 5-fold cross-validation over all images. In cross-validation, ResNet50 and ViT-B/16 reached 92.12% and 91.70% accuracy, and a two one-sided tests (TOST) procedure showed equivalence within a +/-3 percentage-point margin (difference +0.43 points, 90% CI -0.33 to +1.18; p < 0.001). The architectures differed mainly in cost: ResNet50 had 3.6 times fewer parameters and 4.3 times fewer operations, 2.5-fold lower single-image CPU latency (69 vs. 177 ms) and 3.8-fold higher GPU throughput. Cross-validated calibration was similar (expected calibration error 0.021 vs. 0.023). An edge-oriented transformer, MobileViT-S, was most accurate on the test split (95.5%) but lost 9.4 points when an acquisition artefact present in some classes (the ScopeGuide inset) was occluded, whereas ResNet50 was not significantly affected. On 500 unseen polyp images with expert masks, both models' Grad-CAM maps focused on the lesion (3.6 times chance). On a de-duplicated external subset of GastroVision (1,998 images), accuracy fell to 78-80% for all three models and differences between them remained small. Accuracy differences between architectures were thus small relative to the effects of shortcut learning and domain shift, whereas efficiency differences were large. An efficient CNN is a defensible default for resource-constrained screening, but no model is deployment-ready without local validation.
  </details>

- **[TurboWAM: Learning What Matters for Fast World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33941)**  `arXiv:2610.33941`  `cs.CV`  
  _Guile Wu, Shuangzhi Li, Xingxin Chen, Chengjie Huang, Sam Hosseini, Tongtong Cao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  World action models have demonstrated promising performance in robot policy learning, yet their high inference latency remains a critical bottleneck for real-world deployment. Recent approaches have sought to improve inference efficiency by reducing test-time future-frame imagination or accelerating action prediction. However, these methods often leverage representations from all video world model layers without explicitly considering their varying relevance to action prediction, leading to redundant computation and suboptimal representation utilization. In this work, we propose TurboWAM, a fast world action model that efficiently aggregates structured world representations conditioned on action predictions. Specifically, TurboWAM introduces a structured world representation aggregation module that adaptively fuses representations from different world model layers according to their relevance to the evolving action denoising state. Furthermore, TurboWAM employs a structured action supervision mechanism that adaptively modulates gradients from action supervision across different world model layers during backward propagation, facilitating world representation learning. During inference, by reducing the number of world model layers executed and caching relevant representations across action denoising steps, TurboWAM substantially reduces computational overhead. Extensive experiments on multiple robot manipulation benchmarks demonstrate that TurboWAM achieves low inference latency while maintaining competitive task success rates compared with state-of-the-art methods.
  </details>

- **[WMEval: Evaluating Interactive World Models Grounded on Physical Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33894)**  `arXiv:2610.33894`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lanxiang Hu, Yixin Huang, Abhilash Shankarampeta, Zilin Dai, Yuyuan Wu, Kun Qian, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive world models hold immense potential for high-stakes applications in robotics, game engines, and immersive digital twins, yet existing benchmarks primarily focus on perceptual similarity rather than the physical-causal coherence essential for real-world utility. We introduce WMEval, a comprehensive benchmark that evaluates interactive world models against Unity-based physical simulations. By leveraging Unity as a high-fidelity ground-truth oracle, we shift evaluation beyond perceptual visual assessment toward the objective state-based verification required for downstream applications. Spanning 9 diverse scenes, WMEval assesses action controllability, scene and character consistency, prompt adherence, kinematics, fluid mechanics, physical causality, and lighting. By executing matched action sequences in both the simulator and the world model, WMEval enables evaluation at both the perceptual level and the physical-causal level, using simulator-derived states as reference. Experiments on state-of-the-art models reveal significant room for improvement in dimensions that remain hidden by standard perceptual metrics but are vital for real-world deployments. Our code is available at:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Semantic Concept Steering Breaks the Explanation Drift Loop in Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33877)**  `arXiv:2610.33877`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yehonatan Elisha, Oren Barkan, Noam Koenigstein_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Catastrophic forgetting in continual learning (CL) manifests not only as accuracy degradation but also as \emph{explanation drift}: models maintain predictive accuracy while silently shifting attention from diagnostic features to spurious correlations. This work identifies and resolves a previously overlooked structural flaw in explanation-aware CL: pixel-level saliency regularization derives supervision targets from the model's own frozen saliency maps, creating a \emph{self-referential drift loop} that actively reinforces spurious attention across tasks. We introduce \textbf{C$^3$L} (Concept-Consistent Continual Learning), which breaks this loop by anchoring regularization to class-consistent semantic concepts (e.g., ``curved beak'', ``striped pattern'') discovered offline, independent of model state, providing (i) target independence from model drift, and (ii) cross-instance semantic consistency across every instance of a class. We identify that our concept-based objective, combined with approximate LRP relevance conservation, induces a \emph{crowding-out effect}: since concept and background regions compete for the relevance of the same prediction, enforcing high relevance within concept regions implicitly suppresses spurious background correlations without explicit penalization. \method does not require human-annotated concept sets, as it employs a fully automated concept discovery and spatial grounding. Extensive evaluation across five benchmarks and over 20 baselines demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in accuracy, forgetting, explanation quality, spurious correlation mitigation, and out-of-distribution robustness.
  </details>

- **[Road Infrastructure Perception for Autonomous Driving: Taxonomy, Representations, Tasks, and Challenges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33758)**  `arXiv:2610.33758`  `cs.CV`  
  _Guoyang Zhao, Weiqing Qi, Kai Zhang, Zhihai Bi, Tianshuai Hu, Zeying Gong, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Road infrastructure perception is a fundamental component of autonomous driving, as it recovers the structured road elements that define roadway geometry, delimit operational space, and encode traffic regulations. Although individual tasks such as lane detection, drivable-space estimation, traffic-sign recognition, and online map construction have been extensively studied, the field remains fragmented across task definitions, sensing assumptions, output representations, datasets, and evaluation protocols. This survey provides a function-oriented synthesis of road infrastructure perception. We first clarify its scope and organize existing studies into three complementary categories: geometric-structural, functional-spatial, and regulatory-semantic infrastructure. Based on this taxonomy, we review sensing modalities, scene representations, representative methods, benchmarks, and evaluation protocols, emphasizing the field's evolution from image-space recognition toward metric, vectorized, topology-aware, occupancy-based, and rule-grounded scene modeling. We further examine how representation choices affect evaluation and downstream usability, revealing limitations of current benchmarks in relation reasoning, temporal consistency, robustness, and planning-oriented assessment. Finally, we discuss open challenges and future directions toward unified infrastructure representation, open-environment generalization, relation- and rule-aware reasoning, and downstream-oriented evaluation. By connecting tasks, representations, and evaluation under a common functional framework, this survey aims to establish road infrastructure perception as a coherent foundation for map-compatible and behavior-relevant autonomous-driving scene understanding.
  </details>

- **[LENS: Likelihood Evaluation and Neighbor Smoothing for Attribute-Based Person Retrieval on Unseen Cameras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33641)**  `arXiv:2610.33641`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nguyen Dang Duy Tan, Vo Thanh Nguyen, Linh T. T. Tran, Huynh Nguyen Phat_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Attribute-based person retrieval ranks surveillance images from a binary query of semantic attributes, but mean Average Attribute Degree of Match (mADM) drops on unseen cameras as predicted probabilities shift. Common pipelines rank gallery images by their L1 distance to the query, which penalizes a confident single-attribute mismatch no more than small uncertainties across many attributes, while post-hoc recalibration fitted on source validation data fails to transfer because the per-attribute maps fitted on source data do not match those of new cameras. We propose LENS (Likelihood Evaluation and Neighbor Smoothing), combining fine-tuned DINOv3 and CLIP models with a distilled student. LENS regularizes mutually exclusive attribute groups with group cross-entropy in training, scores gallery images by the log-likelihood of the exact query, and smooths gallery logits over nearest neighbors using DINOv3 appearance features from the same forward pass. On held-out datasets, all three changes raise mADM: the likelihood score improves all six retrieval metrics for the main ensemble and mADM for all 48 development models, and training-free kNN smoothing improves all 46 tested development models. On the hidden test set of the UPAR 2027 benchmark, LENS reaches 68.17 mADM.
  </details>

- **[UniQuery: Unifying Vision Models with a Joint Query Head for Cross-Domain Pedestrian Attribute Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33639)**  `arXiv:2610.33639`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nguyen Dang Duy Tan, Linh T. T. Tran, Huynh Nguyen Phat, Vo Thanh Nguyen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pedestrian Attribute Recognition (PAR) predicts semantic attributes of individuals, such as gender, age, clothing and accessories, and its descriptions support attribute-based person retrieval and person re-identification. However, accurately recognizing fine-grained attributes across domains remains challenging due to occlusion, appearance variations, and shifts in image distributions. Ensembles of independently trained foundation encoders combine their logits with fixed fusion weights, so every attribute uses the same mix and the encoders are never trained together. We propose UniQuery, which instead jointly fine-tunes DINOv3 and CLIP for attribute-specific fusion. It combines two query heads, one for each encoder, with a joint query head whose attribute queries attend to tokens from both encoders, and the prediction is the average of the logits of the three query heads. We evaluate UniQuery on the benchmark of the UPAR Challenge 2027 at the WACV 2027 Real-World Surveillance workshop, where models are trained on PA100k, PETA and Market-1501 with a shared set of attributes. In leave-one-dataset-out experiments, UniQuery outperforms the independent ensemble in Challenge_Avg on both held-out datasets. Ablations show that learning fusion over independently trained encoders does not improve upon logit averaging in the mean over the two held-out datasets, while freezing the encoders lowers the Challenge_Avg of UniQuery. On the hidden test set of the benchmark, UniQuery achieves a Challenge_Avg of 0.8001.
  </details>

- **[ONOT/TONO-Print: A Paired Benchmark for Identity-Document Portrait Quality under Document Production Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33627)**  `arXiv:2610.33627`  `cs.CV`  
  _Alexander Vinogradov_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Identity-document portraits are modified during document production by printing, engraving, and integration into the document template. These transformations may change image texture, contrast, colour, and other visual properties without necessarily changing the underlying biometric or presentation information. In this work, we introduce ONOT/TONO-Print, a small paired benchmark for evaluating face-image quality models under identity-document production shift. The benchmark is based on ONOT and TONO portraits and contains synthetic variants representing five common production techniques: toner, inkjet, dye sublimation, laser engraving, and thermal transfer. A smaller physical subset contains portraits printed and scanned using inkjet and toner devices. The benchmark is used to compare the behaviour of biometric and ICAO-related face-quality components before and after production. Results show that production can introduce substantial domain shift for some quality estimators, while other models remain comparatively stable.
  </details>

- **[Mix Forcing: Causal Pre-Training via Mixture Attention for Interactive World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33589)**  `arXiv:2610.33589`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zelin Gao, Hao Ouyang, Jiapeng Zhu, Qiuyu Wang, Jingye Chen, Zichen Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While interactive video world models require autoregressive generation, directly adapting pretrained bidirectional video diffusion models into autoregressive ones with teacher forcing can disrupt their visual priors and degrade autoregressive generation quality. In this paper, we introduce Mix Forcing, a causal pre-training paradigm for interactive video world models, implemented with the Mixture-of-Bidirectional-and-Autoregressive (MoBA) attention. Mix Forcing jointly trains a video diffusion model with both teacher forcing and bidirectional training, yielding a strong causal initialization for few-step post-training. In self-attention, Mix Forcing leverages teacher forcing to learn causal next-chunk prediction for autoregressive rollout, while using bidirectional attention to preserve pretrained visual priors. To support online text interaction, Mix Forcing further adopts a chunk-wise cross-attention design that conditions each video chunk on a shared environment prompt and its own local prompt, without exposing future instructions to the current chunk. Following causal pre-training on a 14B image-to-video model, we apply consistency and distribution matching distillation to obtain a four-step autoregressive generator. Experiments on our internal benchmark and public benchmarks, including WBench and SANA-WM-Bench, show that our model achieves state-of-the-art visual quality and timely event following with precise camera control. Notably, our model supports high-fidelity long-horizon generation without quality degradation even over one-hour rollouts. Project page is here:this https URL
  </details>

- **[CADonatello: Towards Real-World Multi-Part 3D CAD Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33575)**  `arXiv:2610.33575`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ahmet Serdar Karadeniz, Dimitrios Mallis, Parsa Akbari Chianeh, Kseniya Cherenkova, Anis Kacem, Djamila Aouada_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing methods for CAD reconstruction from 3D scans achieve strong performance on standard benchmarks, yet their effectiveness often degrades in real-world scenarios where objects consist of multiple parts and scan artifacts such as noise. A key reason is that current CAD reconstruction datasets predominantly contain single-part models and clean geometric inputs, limiting the ability of learned models to generalize to realistic settings. In this work, we introduce CADonatello, a part-aware framework for reconstructing parametric CAD models from real-world 3D scans. Our approach first decomposes the input geometry into structurally meaningful components using a foundation-scale 3D part segmentation model, and then reconstructs each component independently through a cross-section-driven pipeline that performs sketch parameterization followed by an extrusion. To improve the robustness of sketch parameterization to scan-derived cross-sections, we further introduce CC3D Cross-Sections, a dataset of paired rasterized cross-section images from realistic scans and their corresponding parametric sketches derived from aligned CAD models. This dataset enables effective domain adaptation from clean CAD sketch images to scan-derived cross-section images. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed approach improves generalization to noisy scans and enables reliable reconstruction of complex multi-part objects across multiple real-world CAD reverse engineering benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[What Remains OOD? OODConceptBench for Open-World Concept Recognition in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33564)**  `arXiv:2610.33564`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhiran Luo, Zhiyong Yang, Zitai Wang, Sicong Li, Qianqian Xu, Qingming Huang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In the era of large-scale pretrained models, zero-shot generalization has increasingly blurred the boundary between known and unknown concepts. However, current evaluations do not directly reveal which open-world concepts different vision-language models can recognize. To study this problem, we introduce OODConceptBench, a hierarchical benchmark for open-world concept recognition on image-concept pairs. The full benchmark set (L1) contains 30,752 images, and the harder subset (L2) contains 14,285 images. The benchmark covers 560 concept phrases across seven open-world categories. We evaluate 37 vision-language models across release year, model family, and variant. We obtain three main findings. First, target-concept recognition is lower on L2 than on L1 across all 37 evaluated models. Second, later-released models do not necessarily achieve stronger open-world concept recognition. Third, an increase in overall recognition does not imply consistent improvement across all benchmark categories. Together, these results show that OOD remains a challenge even in the era of large-scale vision-language pretraining.
  </details>

- **[GeoLift: Grounding Video Spatial Reasoning in Explicit 3D Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33558)**  `arXiv:2610.33558`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xinglin Li, TingTing Long, Jingzhi Zhou, Jiajing Chen, Jian Yang, Jin Xie_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video spatial reasoning requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to understand how objects are arranged and related across viewpoints. While these models can recognize objects in individual frames, integrating these observations to solve complex spatial tasks remains challenging. We examine route planning as a representative task and observe that existing models become substantially less accurate as the number of required turns increases, with several models achieving accuracy close to random guessing on routes requiring three or more turns. This finding suggests that recognizing landmarks alone is insufficient: models must also connect observations across viewpoints and track how landmarks are arranged in a shared space. Motivated by this observation, we introduce GeoLift, a training-free framework that uses feed-forward 3D reconstruction to map objects detected in individual frames into a shared metric 3D scene. GeoLift combines a frozen MLLM for 2D grounding with pretrained geometry models for scene reconstruction. It then uses shared geometric regions, called superpoints, to associate observations of the same object across views without a learned matcher. The reconstructed 3D map enables explicit computation of spatial relations and multistep routes, and provides geometric evidence for estimating distances and sizes. With all model weights frozen, GeoLift improves Qwen3-VL-4B's average score on VSI-Bench from 55.4% to 64.1%, including an increase in route-planning accuracy from 30.9% to 53.6%, and achieves 59.8% accuracy on MindCube. These results show that connecting frame-level object grounding through a shared 3D map can improve complex spatial reasoning without task-specific training.
  </details>

- **[Cross-Scale Sparse Attention for Referring Segmentation in Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33551)**  `arXiv:2610.33551`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuxi Li, Lu Si, Xia Liao, Yidong Shen, Zheng Wei, Jun Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-resolution remote sensing (RS) imagery provides fine spatial detail over large areas, making it essential for fine-grained scene understanding. Referring Remote Sensing Image Segmentation (RRSIS) aims to segment regions described by natural language, yet many existing methods rely on sequential alignment--fusion pipelines with limited joint modeling of cross-scale visual hierarchies and linguistic dependencies. Moreover, dense attention mechanisms struggle with the sparsity and wide spatial dispersion of RS targets, while current datasets offer limited structured evaluation of multi-target references, multi-hop relational reasoning, and multi-granularity descriptions, limiting both model development and evaluation. To address these challenges, we propose a Cross-Scale Sparse Attention Model (CSSAM), which aligns multiscale visual features with complex referring expressions through two key modules. The Cross-scale Modality-aligned Fusion Module (CMFM) performs unified cross-scale cross-modal fusion, while the Dynamic Multimodal Sparse Hybrid Attention (DMSHA) selectively attends to informative visual--textual tokens, reducing dense visual interactions. Furthermore, we introduce M$^{3}$RRSIS-D, a RRSIS dataset explicitly designed around three core reasoning dimensions: multi-target (M$^{1}$), reference-dependent multi-hop (M$^{2}$), and multi-granularity (M$^{3}$) expressions. Extensive experiments on M$^{3}$RRSIS-D and two public RRSIS benchmarks demonstrate that CSSAM achieves competitive performance across accuracy, fine-grained reasoning, and high-IoU localization metrics, validating its effectiveness for complex semantic reasoning in realistic RS scenarios. Code, dataset, and model weights will be released upon acceptance.
  </details>

- **[Qwen-Image-PE: Learning Visual Design for Text-to-Image Prompt Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33472)**  `arXiv:2610.33472`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zekai Zhang, Yanran Zhang, Shengming Yin, Yan Shu, Zhening Liu, Bingxuan Zhao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Learning visual design is essential for prompt enhancement (PE) to turn underspecified user requests into coherent visual specifications for text-to-image (T2I) generation. Existing methods preserve user intent and improve overall visual appeal, with less explicit emphasis on fine-grained design dimensions. To address this gap, we introduce Qwen-Image-PE, which learns visual design through two-stage training. Supervised fine-tuning with Caption-Based Reverse Distillation (CRD) uses dense captions of well-designed images as design supervision. Reinforcement learning with Groupwise Design Reward (GDR) jointly compares all candidate images for the same request to refine lighting, composition, layout, and detail. On Qwen-Image-Bench with Qwen-Image-2.1, Qwen-Image-PE-397B improves over original requests by 7.81 points, while Qwen-Image-PE-9B outperforms all evaluated PE baselines. Further evaluations show gains in visual design, aesthetics, image quality, and human preference. We find that PE scales along three axes: training data, model size, and information density, with sufficient information density as a prerequisite for effective enhancement. Moreover, we demonstrate that Qwen-Image-PE generalizes to four additional T2I backbones, even surpassing native prompt enhancers on HunyuanImage-2.1 and FLUX 3 Image Klein. We release Qwen-Image-PE-9B atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[How Robust Are CNNs and Vision Transformers for Alzheimer's MRI Classification Across Datasets?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33416)**  `arXiv:2610.33416`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hafija Aktar, Molly Croes, Frank S. Menniti, Abdeltawab Hendawi_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accurate assessment of Alzheimer's disease stages from MRI is important for understanding disease progression and supporting the development of computer-aided analysis tools. Deep learning models have shown promising results for Alzheimer's disease classification from MRI. However, when evaluated on single datasets, it is difficult to determine whether the observed performance reflects consistent architectural behavior or dataset-specific effects. In this work, we present a multi-dataset benchmarking framework with standardized preprocessing and evaluation. We compare five architectures: ResNet-50, ReXNet-150, Swin Transformer, ViT-Base, and ViT-Large. The models are evaluated across three MRI datasets representing severe class imbalance, train-test distribution mismatch, and large-scale augmentation. The results show broadly consistent performance patterns across datasets, with ViT-Large achieving the best performance on all three datasets and reaching up to 99.84% accuracy and 99.86% macro F1. Performance differences between architectures are most pronounced under distribution mismatch and considerably smaller in the augmented-data setting. Across datasets, lower class-level performance is observed primarily among adjacent early-stage categories, particularly Very Mild and Mild impairment. These findings highlight the importance of evaluating deep learning architectures across multiple data conditions rather than relying on single-dataset performance.
  </details>

- **[Bidirectional Cross-frequency Domain Interaction Network for UHD Low-Light Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33402)**  `arXiv:2610.33402`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bo Fu, Hai Chi, Liyan Wang, Shilin Fu, Wei Wang, Kin-Man Lam, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing Ultra-High-Definition (UHD) low-light image enhancement (LLIE) methods typically rely on either spatial- or frequency-domain modeling, or independently process different frequency components for efficient restoration. However, they often fail to jointly exploit complementary spatial--frequency information and the inherent relationship between illumination and structural information across frequencies, leading to inconsistent illumination, residual noise, and detail artifacts. In this paper, we propose a novel Bidirectional Cross-frequency Domain Interaction Network (BiFreqNet) for UHD LLIE. Specifically, BiFreqNet incorporates a Dual-domain Feature Interaction (DFI) module into the multi-scale representations of a pretrained variational autoencoder (VAE) to perform reciprocal enhancement between low- and high-frequency features. DFI consists of a Spatial and Spectral-Aware Low-Frequency Enhancement (S$^2$LFE) block and a Cross-Frequency Enhancement (CFE) block. S$^2$LFE exploits high-frequency structural cues to guide low-frequency restoration and jointly models spatial and spectral information to recover global exposure and spatially varying illumination while suppressing sensor noise. CFE further leverages the enhanced low-frequency contextual information to guide adaptive high-frequency refinement, enabling faithful reconstruction of structures and fine details. Extensive experiments on four general and UHD LLIE benchmarks demonstrate that BiFreqNet achieves competitive or superior performance compared with state-of-the-art methods.
  </details>

- **[Tight Learned Event-Inertial Odometry (TLEIO): a Transformer-Based Architecture for End-to-End Motion from Event Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33356)**  `arXiv:2610.33356`  `cs.CV`  
  _Matteo Cascone, David Jensen, Alessandro Pirini, Angelo Stefanini, Pietro Venè, Marta Tintoré Gazulla, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Event cameras provide microsecond temporal resolution, high dynamic range, and sparse asynchronous measurements, making them compelling for odometry under fast motion and challenging illumination. Yet, accurate event-based state estimation remains difficult: existing systems often use events as a complement to frames or rely on feature tracking, correspondence search, and optimization-heavy backends, limiting the efficiency promised by event sensing. We introduce Tight Learned Event-Inertial Odometry (TLEIO), a compact filter-based pipeline that learns short-window relative motion directly from voxelized event streams and tightly fuses the resulting constraints with IMU propagation. Its learned front-end, EventsFormer, predicts local displacements and per-axis uncertainty from event voxels in a single forward pass, while a stochastic-cloning EKF fuses these overlapping predictions with IMU propagation, producing metric trajectories without explicit feature tracks and pose optimization at inference time. The network is trained directly from pose displacements, without requiring event-flow labels. We evaluate TLEIO on the TartanAir/TartanEvent benchmark and on real DAVIS240c sequences. On TartanAir, TLEIO reduces average ATE from 2.26 m to 1.10 m over the IMU-only TLIO baseline, while achieving up to 48.9% lower runtime than optimization-based event odometry methods.
  </details>

- **[Compression of Vision Foundation Models for Retinal OCT via Growing-Neural-Gas Codebooks and Graph-Guided Masked Image Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33340)**  `arXiv:2610.33340`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ali Yavari, Tilman Schmoll, Rainer A. Leitgeb, Wolfgang Drexler, Thomas Schlegl_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision foundation models for retinal imaging are large and computationally demanding, limiting point-of-care deployment. We ask how much of such a model's downstream utility survives compression into a smaller network. We propose a topology-aware discretization framework that distills a continuous foundation model into a discrete codebook, then uses it as the reconstruction target for masked image modeling (MIM). It combines gradient-free codebook learning via Growing Neural Gas, which yields an adjacency graph over the codes, with a graph-distance soft cross-entropy loss that replaces one-hot MIM targets with topologically graded supervision. With student pretraining on 383 public Duke iAMD optical coherence tomography (OCT) volumes, the pipeline distills an 86M-parameter teacher into a 22M-parameter student with at least 3.6x fewer FLOPs. The student performs close to, and in some settings above, its teacher across three classification and two segmentation benchmarks, with the magnitude and direction of the difference depending on the teacher, task, metric, and student capacity. On the external OLIVES benchmark, it surpasses its MIRAGE teacher on every metric (98.06% vs. 95.22% balanced accuracy). It works with two Vision Transformer (ViT-Base) teachers, retinal (MIRAGE) and natural-image (DINOv3); with the out-of-domain teacher, the student outperforms it on retinal-layer segmentation, indicating that the added value of retina-specific pretraining is task-dependent: largest for segmentation, while a general-domain teacher is competitive for classification. These results yield compact retinal models potentially suitable for deployment, transferring across OCT datasets, devices, and clinical tasks, including three datasets not used in the student's pretraining.
  </details>

- **[World-in-Context: Interactive Video World Modeling with In-Context Conditioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33169)**  `arXiv:2610.33169`  `cs.CV`  
  _Harold Haodong Chen, Rui Peng, Rongjin Guo, Lingteng Qiu, Kejie Qiu, Chao Xu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive video world models require coordinated control over viewing perspectives, character actions, and camera motion. Existing methods typically leave view modes implicit in text captions, conflate action with camera motion, and inject controls through fragmented architectural pathways that capture interdependencies only indirectly. In this work, we present World-in-Context, an interactive world modeling framework powered by In-Context Conditioning (ICC). Rather than relying on specialized conditioning pathways, World-in-Context unifies view, action, and camera controls into explicit tokens contextualized directly within a shared self-attention space. Our formulation reveals that (i) explicit view tokens disentangle conflicting dynamics and unlock emergent view switching from single-view data, while (ii) action and camera inputs play complementary roles in governing local responsiveness and global stability. To preserve temporal continuity across execution intervals, we further introduce Interaction-Transport RoPE (IT-RoPE), parameterizing temporal attention with accumulated control transitions. On WBench, World-in-Context-5B achieves navigation performance on par with 22B EchoWM ($81.5$ vs. $81.6$) and attains a $93.8\%$ perspective-switching success rate, substantially outperforming existing 5B baselines ($18.8\%$). Controlled analyses further demonstrate compounding advantages of World-in-Context under richer conditioning mixtures, favorable data efficiency, and natural applicability to causal rollouts.
  </details>

- **[WorldCast: World-Consistent Character Video Generation with Large-Angle Camera Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33135)**  `arXiv:2610.33135`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lei Cao, Yu Liu, Wenhua Liao, Peng Zhang, Fei Wang, Hao Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Precise camera control is essential for cinematic character video generation, yet existing methods struggle to follow large-angle trajectories with combined rotation, translation, and radius variation from arbitrary initial azimuths. Such viewpoint changes also reveal character regions unseen in the initial frame, causing geometric distortions, appearance degradation, and identity drift. To systematically study these coupled challenges, we introduce CLACT, a large-scale Character-centric dataset and benchmark covering diverse Large-Angle Camera Trajectories, with accurate camera geometry and multi-view character observations for training and evaluation. We further propose WorldCast, a framework for world-consistent character video generation under diverse large-angle camera motions. Specifically, we back-project the initial frame into a 3D point cloud using estimated depth, align the point-cloud scale with the target camera trajectory, and render it along the trajectory to obtain explicit geometric guidance and coverage masks. To complement the incomplete character information, we introduce Geometry-Aligned Memory Attention (GAMA). Using relative camera poses estimated jointly from the initial frame and multi-view character anchors, we express anchor and target-view geometry in a common coordinate frame. GAMA uses this geometric alignment to guide the retrieval and injection of view-relevant character features during generation. Evaluations across diverse large-angle camera trajectories demonstrate accurate trajectory following while preserving character geometry, appearance, and identity.
  </details>

- **[EKT-Router: Expert-Evidence Alignment for Embodied Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33044)**  `arXiv:2610.33044`  `cs.CV`  
  _Weilin Ruan, Yingjie Xu, Yangkai Wei, Bohan Zhou, Shuaibo Li, Harold Haodong Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Embodied vision-language models must answer questions from partial, evolving observations. Their success depends on both the reasoning capability selected and the evidence available to it. Expert routers adapt internal computation, while tool-augmented systems acquire external evidence; choosing these independently can leave a suitable expert without the evidence it needs. We introduce EKT-Router, a bounded framework that jointly selects a capability expert, optional knowledge, and perceptual tools. Outcome-derived signatures represent the overlapping strengths of General, Spatial Reasoning, and Spatiotemporal experts. A joint utility scores complete expert-evidence routes under a call budget. After acquisition, a packet verifier admits valid evidence with positive predicted expert-conditioned gain. The selected expert then answers once, reverting to the unchanged observation if all packets are rejected. Across 6 embodied QA benchmarks, EKT-Router achieves a mean score of 61.4, the highest among the compared systems, and averages 74.7 on 10 general vision-language benchmarks. With all external evidence disabled, question-conditioned expert selection alone improves over the best fixed expert by 1.75 points. Ablations show that learned routing is the dominant component and that knowledge and perception are complementary.
  </details>

- **[Nemotron 3.5 Bolt 74B-A9B 24GB: A Hybrid Vision-Language Model for Local AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33039)**  `arXiv:2610.33039`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ganesh Ajjanagadde, Chris Alexiuk, Luke Alonso, Kumar Anshuman, Maor Ashkenazi, Nave Assaf, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce Nemotron 3.5 Bolt 74B-A9B 24GB, a hybrid Mamba-Transformer vision-language model that optimizes speed and intelligence per GB of memory used for local agentic workloads. Derived from the Nemotron 3.5 Super VL 120B-A12B model family, Nemotron 3.5 Bolt 74B-A9B 24GB combines Star Elastic compression with 2-bit Quantization-Aware Distillation for memory-constrained deployment. The model is optimized for reasoning, agentic tool use, and web & terminal interactions. We also trained a LiLiCorr speculative drafter that is released alongside the model to reach our target inference speed. The resulting model achieves a higher accuracy than leading models with similar memory footprints and speed across many benchmarks for general agentic capabilities (e.g., GDPVal v2), web browsing (e.g., BrowseComp), and terminal use (e.g., Terminal-Bench 2.1), with a relatively high TPS of 84 on RTX Spark that is comparable to models with much lower active parameter counts and more than sufficient for long-horizon agentic applications.
  </details>

- **[Intuition with Evidence: Adaptive Dual Memory for Multimodal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33019)**  `arXiv:2610.33019`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qiuhe Hong, Jingya Wang, Cuzhen Geng, Shenyao Peng, Munan Ning, Yuyang Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As multimodal agents engage in sustained interactions, long-term memory serves two distinct but complementary functions: fine-grained evidence recall and internalized interpretation for global understanding. Although episodic and latent memory address these demands through explicit historical records and internalized experience, neither paradigm alone can accommodate the diverse memory affinities across tasks, owing to limitations in episodic evidence coverage and integration, and in latent factual grounding. To bridge this divide, we propose a dual-memory framework, unifying a model-native latent memory as an intuitive prior with a multi-indexed episodic memory for exact evidence reconstruction via retrieval-conditioned joint alignment. At inference, the framework adaptively composes these memories through a training-free routing mechanism driven by Jensen-Shannon Divergence (JSD) sensitivity and confidence proxies for query-dependent memory affinity. Extensive evaluations across multimodal benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, outperforming single-paradigm baselines overall (+4.67\% over vanilla Episodic), with further analysis highlighting episodic strengths in evidence-localized tasks and latent contributions to integrating dispersed information and compensating for imperfect retrieval.
  </details>

- **[AnyGameRenderer: Entity-Consistent Generative Rendering Across Games and an Entity-Level Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31466)**  `arXiv:2610.31466`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ruicong Liu, Liangyang Ouyang, Fengbo Lan, Zhixiang Wang, Kaipeng Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  AI coding agents can now build small playable games in minutes, but the results usually look like programmer art: gray blockouts, colored geometric primitives, and flat semantic blobs. We present AnyGameRenderer, a video-to-video generative renderer that turns such footage into high-quality visuals with characters, motion detail, and effects. Throughout, it keeps the layout, camera, and every entity's position and timing intact. Our AnyGameRenderer generalizes across a wide range of genres that agents build, such as tower defense, auto-battlers, top-down survivors, turn-based battles, kart racing, and crowd runners. AnyGameRenderer takes plain video as its game-state input, with no complex representations such as engine G-buffers, per-object class labels, or low-poly proxies. Gray blockouts, colored primitives, semantic maps, and wireframes all work as input. It also preserves entity correspondence: group counts stay correct, and entities appear and disappear on the right frame. We implement AnyGameRenderer by LoRA fine-tuning MiniMax-H3 ref2va, a 33B reference-video-conditioned video generation model. Training uses ~10k paired clips from three sources: (i) multi-stream rendering data from 38 agent-made games; (ii) two-stream rendering data from Unreal Engine, with programmatically controlled crowds, count changes, and entity scales; and (iii) real gameplay recordings, with pairs reconstructed via 3D-box detection. To evaluate the entity correspondence, we propose an entity-level benchmark of 59 held-out cases with 753 entities, scored against ground-truth boxes. Compared with the base model, AnyGameRenderer better preserves entity correspondence: temporal match rises from 50.9% to 62.9%, missed count changes fall from 34.7% to 21.3%, and fewer entities are hallucinated or left unrendered. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[WeVisOne-VL: Unified Visual Perception from Objects to Pixels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31329)**  `arXiv:2610.31329`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tianming Liang, Junyi Pan, Yuxi Li, Canmiao Fu, Zhipeng Huang, Chong Sun, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing vision-language models (VLMs) remain unreliable for fine-grained visual perception. In this report, we present WeVisOne-VL (WeChat Vision All-in-One), a powerful VLM that unifies object-level and pixel-level perception through structured next-token prediction. It supports multiple visual tasks including object detection, referring localization, counting, OCR, segmentation, and matting, enabling localization at any user-specific granularity, from points and boxes to masks and alpha mattes. Architecturally, we integrate a lightweight pixel decoder into the VLM via a special <OBJ> token to generate instance-level pixel outputs. We train the model with a three-stage curriculum SFT followed by mixed-task RL. Across over 30 benchmarks, WeVisOne-VL demonstrates leading performance on both object-level and pixel-level tasks, while maintaining robust rejection for object-absent queries. By releasing WeVisOne-VL to the open-source community, we hope to advance VLMs from broad visual understanding toward truly reliable perception systems for real-world deployment.
  </details>

- **[ProactEgoMem: Benchmarking Proactive Memory in Streaming Egocentric Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30089)**  `arXiv:2610.30089`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ruotong Liao, Meikel Kokowski, Xiaoxiao Sun, Yongqin Xian, Laura Bravo Sánchez, Shiye Su, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Today's multimodal assistants are typically prompted to interpret the visual evidence presented to them. To offload the cognitive burden of remembering across long horizons, wearable assistants should instead proactively acquire and maintain useful memory as the user's surroundings evolve. Doing so on the user's behalf requires identifying what matters, understanding the scene in real time, and initiating memory maintenance in the background while the user stays focused on their activities. We introduce ProactEgoMem, a benchmark that evaluates proactive memory in streaming egocentric video. Three tasks ground the evaluation in ambient environmental needs: Information Capture (IC) preserves transient yet dense visual information, State Tracking (ST) follows changing object states, and Location Tracking (LT) records object movements. The benchmark defines its protocol at two levels of proactiveness: the Notification Level asks for a single timely notification that satisfies the user's intent, while the deeper Intervention Level asks the agent to maintain memory on the user's behalf through an active start-to-stop tool lifecycle. Parallel ProactEgoMem-Daily and ProactEgoMem-Sci tracks contain 300/100 clips covering daily and scientific use cases, paired with 1000/500 questions in ProactEgoMem-QA, which measures the quality of the proactively acquired memory. Across proprietary, open-source, video-language, and streaming baselines, even the strongest models fail to capture memory both timely and accurately. ProactEgoMem provides a rigorous evaluation foundation for proactive multimodal assistance and for video agents that co-exist with their users.
  </details>

- **[PhyMo: Learning Physical Dynamics with Accurate and Continuous Motion from Multi-View Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29107)**  `arXiv:2610.29107`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shangjia Liu, Jinxi Li, Siyuan Zhou, Bo Yang, Bing Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recovering dynamic 3D scene geometry, appearance, and the physical motion states that govern scene evolution from multi-view videos is important yet challenging. A key difficulty is that appearance can be deceptive. Under rendering supervision alone, a model may fit the observed images by changing color or opacity to compensate for inaccurate deformation, rather than recovering the true evolving geometry and motion. This issue is especially severe in scenes with fast or locally complex motion, where inaccurate trajectories can still produce plausible images over the observed frames, but lead to unreliable reconstruction and discontinuous future prediction. In this paper, we propose PhyMo, a framework for learning evolving dynamics from multi-view videos. We introduce a Motion-Aware Trajectory Representation that drives the model to recover accurate motion from deformation, rather than relying on appearance shortcuts to explain observed motion. On top of this, we propose a Continuous Motion Evolution module with bidirectional kinematic continuity constraint to promote temporally consistent evolution of displacement, velocity, and acceleration. Together, these designs yield geometrically faithful interpolation and continuous motion extrapolation. Experiments on four dynamic scene benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance.
  </details>

- **[StreamEval: Evaluating Interactive Streaming Video Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28402)**  `arXiv:2610.28402`  `cs.CV`  
  _StreamEval Team_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce StreamEval with four key features: (1) First interactive streaming video generation benchmark. (2) Fully open source. Benchmark data, evaluation code, protocols, and scoring rules are publicly available. (3) Comprehensive coverage. Five tasks cover avatar dialogue, avatar performance, world generation, instruction editing, and reference editing, with 300 scripted cases lasting 15 to 300 seconds. Ten metrics assess task execution, state consistency, visual fidelity, and audiovisual coordination. (4) Monthly updates. We will evaluate new and updated models and publish leaderboard results monthly. Results from 37 models show that high visual quality and identity retention do not necessarily coincide with reliable request execution or sustained state consistency. The leaderboard is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[MTEdit: Packed Trajectories Beat Rollouts for Multi-Turn Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23751)**  `arXiv:2610.23751`  `cs.CV`  
  _Houyuan Chen, Fanzhou Wang, Xiangyu Fan, Songlin Yang, Zhitao Yang, Lvmin Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-turn image editing aims to modify an image according to a sequence of instructions, making it better aligned with human-AI collaborative creation than single-turn editing. However, constructing high-quality multi-turn training data remains challenging, as conventional sequential rollout accumulates errors and introduces visual-quality drift across editing turns. To address this challenge, we introduce MTEdit, a multi-turn image editing dataset built through a pipeline centered on packed trajectory canvas (PTC) generation. By jointly generating multiple editing states within a unified visual context, this pipeline reduces visual-quality drift across turns. Covering both step-wise and context-aware editing scenarios, MTEdit comprises 500K multi-turn image editing trajectories, with up to 8 turns and an average of 5.38 turns per trajectory, making it one of the most extensive datasets for multi-turn image editing. We further introduce MTEdit-Bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate both scenarios by assessing models' ability to follow editing instructions, maintain content consistency, resist visual-quality drift, and effectively use historical context across turns. Using MTEdit, we fine-tune Qwen-Image-Edit-Causal to obtain Qwen-Image-MTEdit. On MTEdit-Bench, we evaluate a broad range of closed-source and open-source image editing models, including Qwen-Image-MTEdit. Experimental results show that Qwen-Image-MTEdit achieves the best performance among the evaluated open-source models, demonstrating the effectiveness of MTEdit in improving models' multi-turn image editing capabilities.
  </details>

- **[PreciseEdit: Strong Yet Stable Semantic Transport for One-Step Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23535)**  `arXiv:2610.23535`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jingyi Wang, Xiao Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent one-step text-to-image (T2I) models enable efficient image synthesis, opening up new possibilities for interactive Web-based image editing. However, existing one-step editing methods often construct a global transport field through simple vector operations between conditional predictions. While such strong transport can effectively drive the image toward the target semantics, it may be released along unreliable directions or over non-edited regions, leading to structural distortions within the editing region and background drift elsewhere. More importantly, stabilizing the editing process through global smoothing or uniformly weakening the transport field often makes large semantic changes overly conservative. In this work, we revisit one-step image editing from the perspective of the tension between semantic strength and transport stability, and identify a key insight: strong semantic transport is not inherently unstable. What matters is whether it is applied along reliable directions and at reliable spatial locations. Motivated by this insight, we introduce PreciseEdit, a model-agnostic, training-free and inversion-free one-step editing framework that strengthens target-semantic alignment while preserving background fidelity and structural consistency. We further propose a dual-noise-scale role decomposition strategy: a higher-noise scale produces the strong target-directed transport used for actual editing, whereas a lower-noise scale jointly serves as a probe for directional agreement and spatial structure. Extensive experiments on PIE-Bench demonstrate that PreciseEdit achieves state-of-the-art performance and effectiveness on various editing tasks.
  </details>

- **[MJev-Spatial: System One Spatial Intelligence via Native Attention Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22276)**  `arXiv:2610.22276`  `cs.CV`  
  _Luozheng Qin, Yuhan Zhu, Changhai Zhou, Qian Qiao, Jun Gao, Yuhua Zhou, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spatial intelligence lets an agent perceive and act in the physical world, and many of its judgments must be made at interaction rates: which spatial relation holds, how far an object is, or where to place it. Vision-language models (VLMs) produce these answers by free-form text generation, paying for autoregressive decoding and answer parsing even though the output space is compact: one of a few options, a scalar, or a 2D point. In light of this, we introduce MJev-Spatial, a System 1 multimodal decision model that tackles all three types of spatial understanding tasks within a unified classification framework. Specifically, MJev-Spatial treats each candidate as a class and scores it using the backbone's pretrained query and key projections, without adding parameters. The candidates are the given options for choice tasks, or value bins and image regions refined through coarse-to-fine classification for numerical and point prediction, respectively. At inference, each classification decision takes a single forward pass and generates no answer tokens. To train on heterogeneous spatial data efficiently, we supervise all coarse-to-fine stages jointly with teacher forcing in one forward pass, and combine this with length-aware global batching and cross-GPU load balancing, raising training throughput by 30.8% over the original batching strategy. Built on Qwen3.5-4B and trained on 12.3M question-answer pairs converted from existing spatial datasets, MJev-Spatial achieves the highest overall score (65.64%) among eight tested models, ranks first on seven of twelve benchmarks, and outperforms its unmodified autoregressive base model on all twelve, while running 2.5 times faster than the fastest autoregressive baseline and 5.6 times faster than the base model.
  </details>

- **[ScenePercept: Scenario-Conditioned Perceptual Assessment of Real-World User-Generated Content Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17661)**  `arXiv:2610.17661`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuhan Xia, Yu Chen, Xiang Wang, Shuai Li, Peipie Li, Zekun Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The rapid development of mobile imaging and social platforms has led to explosive growth in user-generated content images, making their systematic and comprehensive perceptual assessment increasingly important. Existing image perception benchmarks are typically task-oriented, curating specific scenarios for quality or aesthetic assessment and applying a uniform perceptual criterion across diverse scenarios. This limits their ability to capture the scene diversity and perceptual complexity of real-world UGC images. To address these limitations, we introduce ScenePercept-Bench, a benchmark designed to reflect the distribution of real-world UGC images, comprising 23,100 images across 21 categories and 105 fine-grained scenes. To account for the influence of scene context on human perception, we design a hierarchical, scene-aware annotation protocol, covering visual quality, aesthetic perception, and scene semantics, with 15 attributes in total. Each image is annotated with an overall score, 15 attribute-level scores, and structured explanatory reasoning. Building on this benchmark, we further introduce Scenario-Conditioned Perceptual Alignment, a training framework that tackles the heterogeneity of perceptual criteria across scenarios. SCPA jointly optimizes absolute score prediction, preference ranking, and cross-scenario calibration, preserving the model general perceptual assessment ability while encouraging it to explore and adapt perceptual criteria across different scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SCPA achieves state-of-the-art performance in UGC image perception and generalizes effectively to established IQA and IAA benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[VisualCoding: Learning Executable Skills for Reliable Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14403)**  `arXiv:2610.14403`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiaqi Liu, Ruinan Jin, Xiaoxiao Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Learning reusable skills can improve visual reasoning agents, yet applying a skill still requires the agent to implement or invoke a procedure and produce the final answer. Errors and hallucinations at these steps can prevent acquired experience from translating into reliable single-run performance. We propose VisualCoding, which combines execution-guided acquisition of reusable visual programs with direct adoption of their answers on applicable inputs. Execution-Guided Program Acquisition composes visual primitives and additional code into family-level solvers, then iteratively tests and revises them on labeled training instances. Scope-Aware Direct Execution runs a retained solver and returns its valid in-scope answer. We evaluate VisualCoding on four models across four benchmarks spanning 69 task families. VisualCoding exceeds the strongest evaluated baseline in mean accuracy by 4.45 percentage points with Gemini-3.1-Pro and by 14.78 points when reusing the frozen Pro-acquired artifacts with Flash-Lite. Ablations support execution-guided acquisition and show that direct execution outperforms textual or callable reuse of the same programs. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[TerraVis: Towards Evaluation of World-Grounded Visual Consistency in Text-to-Image Generation via MLLM Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02959)**  `arXiv:2610.02959`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuai Fu, Jing Gu, Jian Zhou, Zicheng Duan, Gengze Zhou, Qi Wu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent text-to-image models have made substantial progress in photorealism, aesthetics, and text-image alignment. Yet visually appealing images can still violate real-world plausibility, exhibiting malformed object structures, impossible anatomy, physically implausible interactions, or inconsistent spatial relationships. Such failures are not well captured by existing fidelity, aesthetics, preference, or alignment metrics. To address this gap, we introduce TerraVis, a framework for evaluating world-grounded visual consistency in generated images. TerraVis defines a structured taxonomy of world-consistency violations spanning object-, interaction-, and scene-level failures, and employs a multi-stage evaluation framework to identify and quantify them. Given an image, TerraVis first uses an MLLM to assess its eligibility for evaluation, then detects violations across 18 taxonomy-defined types and classifies them as minor or major to derive an overall world-consistency score. Across diverse open-source and proprietary text-to-image models on two widely used benchmarks, TerraVis achieves the strongest correlation with human judgments of world consistency among existing metrics. Our benchmark results further show that models that achieve strong performance on conventional metrics can still exhibit substantial world-consistency failures. These findings highlight world consistency as a complementary evaluation dimension and demonstrate that TerraVis enables systematic quantification, diagnosis, and comparison of such failures. Our code is publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[PhysVista: Benchmarking Physical Intelligence in VLMs via a Perception-Reasoning-Assessment Loop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00559)**  `arXiv:2610.00559`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xinge Peng, Yiting Lu, Tianwu Zhi, Wen Wen, Jianzhao Liu, Xin Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have shown strong multimodal reasoning capabilities, yet whether they truly capture the physical consistency underlying real-world dynamics remains unclear. Existing benchmark paradigms often suffer from fragmented evaluation, focusing on isolated cognitive stages while overlooking the inherent synergy between perception, reasoning, and physical judgment. The lack of a holistic perspective limits the ability to diagnose whether VLMs can reliably serve as evaluators of physical plausibility in generative content. To address these issues, we introduce PhysVista, a benchmark designed to evaluate physical intelligence in VLMs through a closed cognitive loop framework inspired by the human seeing-reasoning-assessment process. PhysVista restores this loop by jointly evaluating physical state perception, physical dynamics reasoning, and physical plausibility assessment. It further distinguishes event-level reasoning and scale-level reasoning to enable fine-grained analysis of physical understanding. In addition, PhysVista incorporates both real-world and AI-generated videos, allowing evaluation across diverse domains and emerging generative scenarios. Extensive experiments across a diverse set of VLMs reveal substantial limitations in physical reasoning and plausibility assessment, highlighting a persistent gap between visual recognition and genuine physical understanding, and pointing toward more principled designs for physically grounded multimodal intelligence.
  </details>

- **[Drag as Evidence: Motion-Grounded Latent Recomposition for Drag-Based Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36755)**  `arXiv:2609.36755`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xinyu Pu, Hongsong Wang, Jie Gui, Pan Zhou_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Modern image editors excel at semantic manipulation and visual synthesis, yet remain limited in precise spatial control, motivating the development of drag-based editing. However, existing drag-based methods often struggle to balance drag accuracy with natural, plausible, and intent-aligned generation. We propose MoRe-Drag, a motion-grounded drag-based editing method. Our key insight is to treat pixel-space warping as coarse motion evidence, and to inject this evidence into the generative sampling trajectory. Specifically, MoRe-Drag performs region-aware latent recomposition over refinement, inpainting, and preservation regions, coupled with stage-adaptive conditioning that progressively shifts from motion-grounded structure formation to semantic refinement. We further support a no-manual-instruction interface by adapting the MLLM-based text encoder for drag-aware instruction inference. Experiments on DragBench-SR and DragBench-DR show that MoRe-Drag substantially improves drag precision over strong base editors and achieves superior drag accuracy among SOTA drag-based methods, while delivering strong semantic consistency and visually realistic results. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[ChatGPT Images 2.5 on Forgery Tasks: Testing Advertised Improvements Against Known Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13617)**  `arXiv:2609.13617`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ankit Raj, Yuxin Zhang, Kidus Zewde, Tommy Duong, Jiaqi Gan, Xingyu Shen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  OpenAI released ChatGPT Images 2.5 on 8 September 2026, advertising more precise local edits, better consistency across edits, more faithful reference products and sharper detail. We evaluate these claims on four forgery tasks with answers fixed in advance: receipt-field alteration, repeated editing, product placement and small-print rendering. GPT-Image-2 provides same-week baselines at a cheaper and a more expensive tier. A limited improvement appears in receipt editing. After alignment, OCR detects changes to surrounding text in 31.7% of Flare outputs, against 44.2% for the cheaper baseline. This gain is concentrated on CORD receipts and sensitive to shifts of a pixel or less; the forged value itself is no more often correct. Repeated editing and fine print show no measurable gain. Product codes become more legible mainly because Images 2.5 draws the product larger. Defence outcomes change little: localisation remains weak for both generations. A detector that flags 68.6% of controlled benchmark images flags only 35.9% of images posted online. Advertised improvements therefore transfer unevenly to the tested forgery capabilities, while substantial detection limitations remain.
  </details>

- **[Sidecar: Training-Free Semantic Reuse for Character-Consistent Free-form Visual Storytelling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27280)**  `arXiv:2608.27280`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sibo Dong, Sarah Adel Bargal_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual storytelling requires generating images that follow a narrative while preserving consistent character identities across frames. In free-form story generation, a character is fully described only when first introduced and is later referred to by a type-level mention or pronoun. Although this setting better reflects natural storytelling, later prompts may omit important identity-related semantics, making character consistency more difficult to maintain. We propose \textbf{Sidecar}, a plug-and-play semantic augmentation module that preserves entity-level information from the initial description and injects the missing semantics into later prompt embeddings. Sidecar requires no additional training and does not modify the architecture of the base diffusion model. Experiments on FreeStoryBench show that Sidecar consistently improves prompt-image alignment and character consistency across multiple SDXL- and FLUX-based baselines, with negligible computational overhead.
  </details>

- **[Vorch-Director: Interactive World Story Model via Noise-Aware Error Rectification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05776)**  `arXiv:2608.05776`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lisai Zhang, Yidi Wu, Qi Liu, Xin Ma, Yang Ding, Gang Yue, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autoregressive continuation provides a natural path toward minute-scale audio-visual generation by repeatedly extending a short-window generator conditioned on previously generated video and audio. However, models are trained on clean ground-truth histories, while inference relies on their own generated histories, where accumulated errors cause identity drift, over-smoothing, and audio-visual desynchronization.
